15. **Retry classification?** 429/5xx/connect yes; 4xx, refusal, truncation, mid-stream timeout no.
16. **Backoff jitter?** Full jitter, always â€” without it you build a thundering herd.
17. **Retries vs semaphore?** Prefer admission control; retries add tail latency exactly when you are overloaded.
18. **Who owns conversation state?** The server. Providers are stateless.
19. **Quadratic cost in chat?** Resending full history every turn is O(nÂ²); window or summarize.
20. **Compaction trigger?** Projected next-request tokens over a fraction of the window, minus output reserve.
21. **Summary style?** Factual and entity-focused, cumulative â€” state, not narrative.
22. **Prompt anatomy?** Role, context, task, constraints, output contract â€” stable prefix first.
23. **Why stable prefix first?** Cache effectiveness and instruction salience.
24. **Few-shot beats adjectives because?** Examples specify the decision boundary; adjectives gesture.
25. **Which few-shot cases?** Typical, hardest edge, and a negative case.
26. **Prompt versioning?** Version is code, with a changelog and an identity recorded per request.
27. **What is a "recipe"?** Prompt + model + schema + params + retrieval config, bound by one id.
28. **Why multiple samples per eval case?** Hosted models are not bit-stable; compare distributions.
29. **Hallucination reduction?** Retrieve real evidence, force a no-answer path, verify citations resolve.
30. **Cheap hallucination check?** Cited numbers must appear in the cited source.
31. **Refusal: never retry?** Correct â€” it is either a policy block or your own prompt's doing.
32. **Truncation: how detected?** `stop_reason`, not string heuristics.
33. **Instruction conflict?** Contradictory rules, or examples that violate rules; fix by auditing, not hoping.
34. **Naive RAG's main weakness?** Retrieval quality, not generation quality. Fix retrieval first.
35. **Embedding choice?** Benchmark 2â€“4 candidates on your own labelled queries; pick on recall then cost.
36. **Changing the embedding model?** Full reindex. Dual-index migration, shadow, cut over, keep v1 warm.
37. **Chunk by tokens, not characters?** Always â€” character sizing mis-sizes with code and non-Latin text.
38. **Parent-child retrieval?** Embed small chunks, return the parent section; precision without losing context.
39. **Table chunking?** One row per chunk, with the header row prepended every time.
40. **`ef_search`?** The runtime recall/latency dial; set it from a measured recall target.
41. **Flat search is fine up to?** Roughly 10^5â€“10^6 vectors depending on dimensions and hardware.
42. **Why hybrid?** Embeddings fail on codes and identifiers; BM25 fails on paraphrase.
43. **Why RRF over score addition?** Rank-based and scale-free â€” no weight tuning of score magnitudes.
44. **Reranking value?** Highest-return quality improvement for most RAG systems; it reorders 50â€“200 candidates.
45. **Query rewrite risk?** Dropping identifiers; fuse with the original query so it degrades gracefully.
46. **HyDE?** Embed a hypothetical answer; fuse with the real query; skip on identifier-heavy corpora.
47. **Context order for Q&A?** Best chunk closest to the question; question after the context.
48. **Relevance floor?** Makes honest declining possible; without it you get confident wrong answers.
49. **RAG metrics?** Recall@k, precision@k, MRR, nDCG; plus groundedness and task success.
50. **Groundedness method?** Decompose into atomic claims, judge entailment per claim.
51. **Guardrail that is a real boundary?** Structural: least privilege, tenant scoping, no ambient credentials.
52. **Confirmation bound to?** An argument hash, single-use, expiring â€” never model-authored copy.
53. **Cost metric that matters?** Cost per successful outcome, not cost per call.
54. **Biggest cost lever?** Prompt caching with a byte-stable prefix, plus not resending history.
55. **When not to use an agent?** When you can specify the correct behaviour as tests â€” write the pipeline.

---

# PHASE 13 FINAL READINESS CHECKLIST

## 13.1 Mental model

- [ ] Can explain, without hedging, why AI work is "software engineering around an unreliable oracle."
- [ ] Know which parts of your fundamentals got *more* important: interfaces, data modelling, idempotency, backpressure, caching, authz, observability, testing.
- [ ] Can name three places a model is the wrong tool, with a cost argument.
- [ ] Distinguish model output that is *structurally* wrong from *semantically* wrong, and know which tooling catches each.

## 13.2 LLM API integration

- [ ] Know the portable subset: messages, roles, content blocks, stop reasons, usage.
- [ ] Branch on every `stop_reason` value, including `max_tokens`.
- [ ] Have written a provider adapter with a capability map and a normalization test.
- [ ] Implement native schema mode, a tool-call fallback, and a bounded repair loop.
- [ ] Implement streaming SSE server + client, with correct `\n\n` framing.
- [ ] Handle cancel, timeout, and partial-result persistence; distinguish them in the UI.
- [ ] Implement selective retry with jitter, a semaphore, and a circuit breaker.
- [ ] Keep conversation state server-side, with a tree, projection, and compaction.
- [ ] Know why client-owned history is disqualifying.

## 13.3 Prompting

- [ ] Write prompts with the five-region anatomy and a stable prefix.
- [ ] Use few-shot examples selected for edge cases, with notes explaining why each exists.
- [ ] Derive the output contract, validator, and types from one schema.
- [ ] Version prompts in code with a changelog; record a recipe id per request.
- [ ] Diagnose hallucination, refusal, truncation, and instruction conflict separately.
- [ ] Run an eval-driven loop: observe, hypothesize, change one thing, measure, gate.

## 13.4 RAG

- [ ] Chunk by structure with token budgets, overlap, breadcrumbs, and headers on table rows.
- [ ] Choose embeddings by benchmark on your own labelled queries.
- [ ] Use hybrid retrieval with RRF, plus a reranker, and measure recall against exact search.
- [ ] Transform queries additively â€” rewrite, decompose, HyDE â€” and measure each independently.
- [ ] Assemble by token budget with a diversity cap, deliberate ordering, and a relevance floor.
- [ ] Compute recall@k, MRR, nDCG, and groundedness; calibrate any judge against humans.
- [ ] Debug by walking stages in order; show the prompt; swap in a known-good chunk.
- [ ] Have incremental indexing with chunk-level vector reuse, soft deletes, vacuum, and a reindex plan.

## 13.5 Tools

- [ ] Write descriptions that state scope, non-scope, argument formats, and return shape.
- [ ] Measure tool and argument accuracy on a labelled set; read the confusion matrix.
- [ ] Implement a bounded loop: step cap, call cap, deadline, budget, loop detection.
- [ ] Authorize every call from the caller's identity; inject tenant; never accept it as an argument.
- [ ] Confirm irreversible and financial actions with server-rendered copy and an argument hash.
- [ ] Know the tool-protocol shape, and that it is not a security boundary.

## 13.6 Agents

- [ ] State what an agent loop is: goal, state, action space, policy, guardrails.
- [ ] Give a concrete reason *not* to use an agent, and a preference for pipelines and routers.
- [ ] Implement plan-then-execute, scratch artifacts, and least-privilege per subtask.
- [ ] Justify multi-agent only with a named coordination problem and a named saving.
- [ ] Implement three-plane guardrails with metrics and an audited bypass.
- [ ] Sandbox model-influenced execution: no credentials, jailed FS, egress allowlist, hard kill.
- [ ] Build a review queue with SLAs, expiry, four-eyes, re-auth, MFA, and audit.

## 13.7 Cost, latency, caching

- [ ] Attribute every call: feature, tenant, model, tokens, cache status, latency, success.
- [ ] Optimize for cost per successful outcome.
- [ ] Structure prompts for prefix caching; snapshot-test the prefix hash in CI.
- [ ] Implement exact and semantic caching with tenant/prompt/model isolation and a tuned threshold.
- [ ] Implement static, difficulty-based, and cascading routing with fallbacks per tier.
- [ ] Queue long work with idempotency keys, heartbeats, partial progress, and budget checks.
- [ ] Measure TTFB and p99; implement designed degradation tiers.
- [ ] Control conversation cost with windowing, compaction, and memory extraction.

## 13.8 Evaluation

- [ ] Test deterministically around the model; evaluate statistically for the model.
- [ ] Build a golden dataset from traffic, bugs, and rejections; store expectations as properties.
- [ ] Slice every report; report confidence intervals; check coverage.
- [ ] Use exact match, field F1, rubric judges with calibration and evidence quoting.
- [ ] Track acceptance, immediate regeneration, edit distance, escalation, abandonment, cost per success.
- [ ] Run A/B tests with a declared primary metric, per-user assignment, guardrails, and novelty awareness.
- [ ] Run deterministic checks in CI and statistical evals nightly; gate on per-case regressions.
- [ ] Maintain a red-team suite covering direct and indirect injection, exfiltration, tool abuse, evasion.

## 13.9 AI coding tools

- [ ] Know interview norms, ask first, and disclose specifics.
- [ ] Know where the review burden lands and how to redistribute it.
- [ ] Brief an agent with context, goal, constraints, verification, and scope; plan first for anything non-trivial.
- [ ] Recognize the failure catalogue: hallucinated APIs, off-by-ones, floating promises, swallowed errors, missing tenant filters, naive datetimes, float money, over- and under-engineering, vacuous tests.
- [ ] Verify by execution: run everything yourself, break a test to confirm it fails, diff-watch migrations and dependencies.
- [ ] Keep authorization, tenancy, money, and crypto paths out of generated changes, or gate them behind a second reviewer.

## 13.10 Capstone self-test

- [ ] Ship one end-to-end AI feature: ingestion, retrieval, orchestration, UI, evaluation, cost, rollback.
- [ ] Be able to answer, for that feature: what is the model, what is the prompt, what is the schema, what is the recipe id, what is the cost per success, what is the eval score per slice, and how do I roll back in under a minute.
- [ ] Be able to name the three things you would change first, with measurements to justify the order.

---

# PHASE 13 COMPLETE

You can now treat a model as what it is: a probabilistic, expensive, slow, remote dependency with a
prompt, a schema, a latency budget, a cost budget, and a distribution of outputs.

You can integrate one behind an adapter with capability flags. You can make it return data you can parse.
You can stream it, cancel it, retry it correctly, and cache it. You can build retrieval that is measured
rather than assumed, and you can debug it stage by stage. You can give it tools it is allowed to use and
not allowed to misuse. You can decide when it is the wrong architecture â€” which is the most valuable skill
of the lot. You can cost it, route it, cap it, and degrade it deliberately. You can prove it is better with
an eval set built from real failures, and you can prove it is *not* better when the slice you forgot to
test collapses. And you can use these tools yourself without shipping code you do not understand.

The through-line of the whole phase: **the parts that matter are the parts that were always the hard
parts.** Interfaces, data, failure modes, authorization, measurement, and verification. The model changed
what you type. It did not change what you are responsible for.
